**Interfaces:**

- Consumes:
  - Task 7's props: `{rows: ContractRow[], onRowsChange, answer: YesNo | null, onAnswerChange, debts: DebtRow[], assets: WizardRow<AssetInfo>[], onValidationChange}`, and `isBlankContract(row)`.
  - Task 8's `AddressFields` and `claimNames.ts`, and Task 5's `YesNoField`.
  - Task 6's constants and gap table.
  - **Assumed shapes, from Task 8's and Task 5's Interfaces. Re-open Task 8's files before coding and change only call sites if they differ:**
    - `claimRowName(name: string | undefined, nameless: string): string`, `addressMissing(a: AddressValue): boolean`, `linkIsLive(id: number | null | undefined, held: {id?: number}[]): boolean`, `fill(template, values)`.
    - `AddressValue = {street_address, street_2, city, state, zip_code, outside_us}`.
    - `AddressFields({prefix, index, value, onChange, required})`, where each input is named `${prefix}${field}${index === undefined ? '' : '_' + index}` with id `field-<name>`, and `onChange(changes: Partial<AddressValue>)`.
    - `CHOOSE_ONE` in `copy.ts`, the label of a blank first option (added in Task 7).
    - `assetLabel(row)` in `claimNames.ts` (Task 8): "Line N: " and the name, a vehicle-shape row named by `[year, make, model]` joined with spaces, the blanks skipped, any other row by `rowName`.
    - `keptRows` and `isBlankContract` from `utils/claimRows.ts` (Task 7).
- Produces:
  - `firstContractGap(rows: ContractRow[], answer: YesNo | null, debts: DebtRow[], assets: WizardRow<AssetInfo>[]): string | null`.
  - `inIdOrder<T extends {id?: number}>(rows: T[]): T[]` in `claimNames.ts`: ids ascending, rows with no id after them in held order (the server's id order, B37).
  - Controls (Task 13 relies on these): `contracts_answer` and `owes_now_<i>` are `YesNoField` radios valued `yes`/`no`; `contract_type_<i>`, `lease_kind_<i>`, `vehicle_asset_<i>` and `owed_debt_<i>` are selects (so `lease_kind_<i>` is a select, valued by the server's key, not a radio), each opening on `CHOOSE_ONE` with value `''`; `vehicle_asset_<i>` options read as `assetLabel` (so a vehicle is named by its make and model, and a substring such as "Corolla" picks it) and `owed_debt_<i>` options as the creditor name, so a substring picks one.
  - Input names (E2E depends on them): `contracts_answer`, `counterparty_name_<i>`, `contract_type_<i>`, `description_<i>`, the address names of Task 8, `lease_kind_<i>`, `vehicle_asset_<i>`, `monthly_payment_<i>`, `owes_now_<i>`, `owed_debt_<i>`. Ids stay `field-<name>`.

**Copy.** Every sentence below is given exactly; add each in the step that adds it.

- `formText.ts` (G's printed words, from `form_b106g.pdf` page 1, extracted with PyMuPDF; no cut unless named):
  - `SCHEDULE_G_LINE_1_QUESTION = 'Do you have any executory contracts or unexpired leases?'`
  - `SCHEDULE_G_NAME_LABEL = 'Person or company with whom you have the contract or lease'`
  - `SCHEDULE_G_PURPOSE_LABEL = 'State what the contract or lease is for'`
  - Above the first constant, a comment: the answers are asked as "No" and "Yes". Cut from No: ". Check this box and file this form with the court with your other schedules. You have nothing else to report on this form." Cut from Yes: ". Fill in all of the information below even if the contracts or leases are listed on Schedule A/B: Property (Official Form 106A/B)." Both are instructions to a person holding the paper form.
- `copy.ts`, DigniFi's:
  - `LEASE_KIND_LABEL = 'Kind of lease'`
  - `VEHICLE_LEASE_LABEL = 'Vehicle you lease'`
  - `VEHICLE_LEASE_HELP = 'The vehicles you listed on the Assets step are here. To add one, go back to that step.'`
  - `LEASE_PAYMENT_LABEL = 'Monthly lease payment'`
  - Each of these three carries `// DRAFT: counsel C-9` above it (C3 in the spec's list is C-9 in §11):
    - `OWES_NOW_QUESTION = 'Do you owe money on this now?'`
    - `OWES_NOW_PICK_LABEL = 'Which amount owed goes with this?'`
    - `OWES_NOW_ADD_HELP = 'If it is not in this list, add it on the Amounts Owed step. Then come back and pick it.'`
  - `GO_TO_AMOUNTS_OWED = 'Go to Amounts Owed'`
  - The reasons for the required boxes, each scored (below the 8.5 limit). The step reason carries no draft mark; the address help carries `// DRAFT: counsel C-9`, since §11's C-9 covers the address help, as `ADDRESS_HELP` on Amounts Owed does:
    - `CONTRACTS_STEP_WHY = 'Your answers here fill in Schedule G, the court form that lists your contracts and leases.'` (6.1). The step shows it under its intro paragraph. Every required control on the step sits under it, so it is the reason for them; no field but the address gets its own.
    - `CONTRACT_ADDRESS_HELP = 'The court uses this address to send the other party notice of your case.'` (5.9). It shows above the row's address boxes.
- The existing inline strings stay as they are: the intro paragraph, the `Type` label, the placeholders (`e.g., Landlord Inc.`, `e.g., Apartment lease, car lease`) and the C-17 comment on the purpose box.
- The row's old name error `Please enter the name of the other party` goes. A row with no name now shows `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_NAME` (Task 6) in its place, on the same `aria-describedby` tie, while `!isBlankContract(row)`.
- Labels `Counterparty Name` and `Description` become the two G constants above.

**How the wizard moves between steps** (read, `IntakeWizard.tsx:543`): `moveToStep(stepNumber)` calls `showStep` and clears Review's reasons. It calls no `update_step` and no save, and the wizard keeps the contract rows in its own state, so the filer's edits wait for them.

- The link is a `Button variant="outline" size="sm"` (type `button`) reading `GO_TO_AMOUNTS_OWED`, under the owed-debt picker. It calls `onGoToStep(6)`: take the number from `WIZARD_STEPS.find((s) => s.key === 'debts')!.number`, never a literal.
- Continue on step 6 then reaches step 7, because `handleNext` goes to `currentStepNumber + 1`. That is B15's trip: add the debt, give its amount, press Continue, pick it.

**Layout decisions** (no ruling covers them; the cost if wrong is named):

- The status region shows the first gap always, including on an untouched step (`CONTRACT_GAP_NO_ANSWER`), as Task 8's does. A Continue that waits with no word said is the anti-pattern the trauma-informed guide names.
- Rows show while the answer is `yes`, or while any non-blank row exists. A `no` with rows must keep showing them, or the filer can't see what `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_BUT_LISTED` names. Cost if wrong: none the filer can see.
- The add button keeps its existing words, `+ Add Contract` (found by `/add contract/i`; Task 7's tests and Task 13 use that name). It shows under `yes` and while rows exist, and a first row is made by pressing it too: no blank row shows with no rows. Pressing No deletes nothing.
- The vehicle picker lists the rows of `assets` with an `id` and `ab_line` `'3'` or `'4'`, leaving out one whose `details.lives_here === 'yes'` (R25, B16), named by `assetLabel` (not `rowName`: a car row has no description, so `rowName` would read "Honda" for every Honda). It always lists the currently picked row, so a pick never vanishes from the box. Cost if wrong: a lease may show a row that is now "I live here", which `CONTRACT_GAP_VEHICLE` doesn't catch (the server's table doesn't either).
- The debt picker lists the rows of `debts` with an `id`, named by `claimRowName(creditor_name, NAMELESS_DEBT)`. A held `owed_debt` that is no longer among them shows the box on "Choose one" and leaves `CONTRACT_GAP_OWED_DEBT` standing.
- Pressing No on "Do you owe money on this now?" sets the row's `owed_debt` to null. Task 6's C13 reads `owed_debt` whether or not `owes_now` is Yes, so a hidden stale pick could otherwise raise the same-vehicle gap.
- A new row is `{_key: 'new-<n>', counterparty_name: '', contract_type: '', description: '', street_address: '', street_2: '', city: '', state: '', zip_code: '', outside_us: false, lease_kind: null, monthly_payment: null, vehicle_asset: null, owes_now: null, owed_debt: null}`. `EMPTY_CONTRACT`'s `'contract'` is already gone and `ContractRow`'s `contract_type` already takes `''` (added in Task 7, B34); this task builds its new row with the other keys.
- Kind select: the blank first option `CHOOSE_ONE` is already there (added in Task 7), then `Object.entries` of the local `CONTRACT_TYPES`. The lease-kind select is the same with a module-private `LEASE_KIND_OPTIONS` (`residential` "Residential", `vehicle` "Vehicle", `other_personal_property` "Other personal property", `other` "Other"). Task 11 moves both lists into `constants/claims.ts`.
- Show the lease-kind select when `contract_type === 'lease'`. Show the vehicle picker and the payment box when it is a lease and `lease_kind === 'vehicle'`. Show the owed-debt picker when `owes_now === 'yes'`.
- The payment box is `type=number min=0 step=.01`, value `monthly_payment ?? ''`, parsed by `parseAmount` (`utils/parseAmount.ts`) as Debts' payment is, so a typed 0 stays 0 and an empty box is null.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing gap tests.** Create `claims/__tests__/contractGaps.test.ts`. A `lease` fixture is a complete vehicle lease with the table's every answer given, so each case changes one thing:

```ts
const assets = [
  { _key: '20', id: 20, ab_line: '3', description: '', details: { year: '2019', make: 'Demo', model: 'Corolla' } },
  { _key: '22', id: 22, ab_line: '4', description: 'Demo Park trailer', details: { lives_here: 'yes' } },
];
const debts = [
  { _key: '30', id: 30, creditor_name: 'Demo Auto Credit', is_secured: true, secured_by: 20, monthly_payment: 389 },
];
const lease = {
  _key: '4', id: 4, counterparty_name: 'Demo Motors', street_address: '1 Demo St', street_2: '',
  city: 'Chicago', state: 'IL', zip_code: '60601', outside_us: false, contract_type: 'lease',
  description: 'Car lease', lease_kind: 'vehicle', vehicle_asset: 20, monthly_payment: 389,
  owes_now: 'no', owed_debt: null,
};
```

  - `it('has no gap on a complete vehicle lease')`: `firstContractGap([lease], 'yes', debts, assets)` is `null`.
  - `it.each` over C1–C13, one row each: `[name, rows, answer, expected]` with the expected the constant itself, from `copy.ts`, filled by `fill`. Mutations:
    - C1 `answer null` → `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_ANSWER`.
    - C2 `([], 'yes')` → `CONTRACT_GAP_YES_BUT_NONE`.
    - C3 `([lease], 'no')` → `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_BUT_LISTED`.
    - C4 `{...lease, counterparty_name: ' '}` → `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_NAME`.
    - C5 `{...lease, city: ''}` → `fill(CLAIM_GAP_ADDRESS, {name: 'Demo Motors'})`, and the same row with `outside_us: true, state: '', zip_code: ''` and a city → `null`.
    - C6 `contract_type: ''` → `fill(CLAIM_GAP_KIND, …)`.
    - C7 `description: ' '` → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_PURPOSE, …)`.
    - C8 `lease_kind: null` → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_LEASE_KIND, …)`.
    - C9 `vehicle_asset: null`, and `vehicle_asset: 99` (a removed row) → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_VEHICLE, …)`.
    - C10 `monthly_payment: null` → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_PAYMENT, …)`, and `monthly_payment: 0` → `null`.
    - C11 `owes_now: null` → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_OWES_NOW, …)`.
    - C12 `owes_now: 'yes', owed_debt: null`, and `owed_debt: 99` → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_OWED_DEBT, …)`.
    - C13 `owes_now: 'yes', owed_debt: 30` (debt 30 is secured, `is_secured: true`, by 20, payment 389, lease payment 389) → `fill(CONTRACT_GAP_SAME_VEHICLE, {name: 'Demo Motors', debt: 'Demo Auto Credit'})`. With `monthly_payment: 0` on the lease, `null`; with debt 30's `is_secured: false`, `null`.
  - `it('names the first gap in the table's order, not the last')`: a row with no name and no city gives `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_NAME`.
  - `it('skips a new row with nothing in it, as the body leaves it out')`: `[{_key: 'new-1', …all blank}]` with answer `'no'` gives `null`, and with `'yes'` gives `CONTRACT_GAP_YES_BUT_NONE` (C2 and C3 count the rows `keptRows(rows, isBlankContract)` returns, never the blank new one).
  - `it('checks rows in id order, a new row last')`: two bad rows, `id: 9` held first and `id: 4` second, return the id-4 row's sentence.
  - `it('keeps a stored row that has been cleared of every answer, since the save sends it under its id')`: `[{_key: '4', id: 4}]` (stored, every answer empty) with `'no'` gives `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_BUT_LISTED`; with `'yes'` it gives `CONTRACT_GAP_NO_NAME`. The server counts and checks the row, so the step's copy must too (X-I6).
  - `it('puts new rows after stored ones in inIdOrder')` in `claimNames`' own test file if Task 8 made one, else here.

**Layout decisions** (no ruling covers them; cost if wrong named):

- H line 2 shows always, under line 1, since H says "Answer every question." It does not depend on line 1's answer.
- The status region shows the first gap always, including on an untouched step (`CODEBTOR_GAP_NO_ANSWER`), as Task 8's does. A Continue that waits with no word said is the anti-pattern the trauma-informed guide names.
- Rows and the add button show while the answer is `yes` or any non-blank row exists, as on Contracts (Task 9). The button keeps its existing words, `+ Add Codebtor` (found by `/add codebtor/i`; Task 7's tests and Task 13 use that name), and a first row is made by pressing it too: no blank row shows with no rows. Cost if wrong: none the filer sees.
- Line 2's second question shows only under `community_lived === 'yes'`. The state, the name and the address show only under both Yes answers (the form's order: state, then "Fill in the name and current address of that person.", then the name, then the address).
- Pressing No on line 2, or on its second question, clears nothing. Task 6's H6 reads those boxes only under both Yes answers, so a hidden value costs nothing, and a filer who flips back keeps what they typed. Cost if wrong: the server stores text for an answer that no longer asks it; Task 14 and 3c5 read it only under both Yes.
- The spouse's address is `AddressFields` with `prefix="community_spouse_"`, no `index`, and `required`. Its value comes from the answers by an adapter local to the step: `spouseAddress(answers): AddressValue` reads `community_spouse_street_address`, `community_spouse_street_2`, `community_spouse_city`, `community_spouse_state`, `community_spouse_zip_code` and `community_spouse_outside_us` (`?? ''` / `?? false`); its `onChange(changes)` writes the same keys back through one `onAnswersChange({...answers, …})`.
- A row's links: `row.links` is `{kind: 'debt' | 'contract', id: number}[]`. A link is live when its id is among the held `debts` (kind `debt`) or `contracts` (kind `contract`) with an `id`, by `linkIsLive`. A checkbox is checked only for a live link. Toggling writes `links` as the row's live links with that one added or removed, so a dead link is dropped by the first toggle and never comes back. Cost if wrong: a dead link stays on the row until a toggle, and the server drops it on save either way.
- Checkboxes group under two subheadings, `CODEBTOR_LINKS_DEBTS` and `CODEBTOR_LINKS_CONTRACTS`, inside one fieldset whose legend is `SCHEDULE_H_COLUMN_2` with `CODEBTOR_LINKS_HELP` below it. A subheading with no rows is left out. With neither, show `CODEBTOR_LINKS_NONE`. Names are `claimRowName(creditor_name, NAMELESS_DEBT)` and `claimRowName(counterparty_name, NAMELESS_CONTRACT)`. None is checked unless the filer checked it.
- A new row is `{_key: 'new-<n>', name: '', street_address: '', street_2: '', city: '', state: '', zip_code: '', outside_us: false, relationship: '', links: []}`; `EMPTY_CODEBTOR`'s `'other'` is already gone and `CodebtorRow`'s `relationship` already takes `''` (added in Task 7, B34); this task builds its new row with the other keys.
- The relationship select's blank first option `CHOOSE_ONE` is already there (added in Task 7); it is optional (no required mark) and keeps the local `RELATIONSHIPS` list, which Task 11 moves.

### Task 11: Review in words, and the choice pins

**Files:**

- Create: `frontend/src/constants/claims.ts` (R23)
- Modify: `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/DebtsStep.tsx` (`DEBT_CATEGORY_META`'s labels, `:28-48`), `ContractsStep.tsx` (`CONTRACT_TYPES` and `LEASE_KIND_OPTIONS`) and `CodebtorsStep.tsx` (`RELATIONSHIPS`): each builds its list from `claims.ts`.
- Modify: `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/ReviewStep.tsx` (debts, contracts and codebtors in words, R19; the three "said no" lines)
- Modify: `frontend/src/pages/IntakeWizard.tsx` (the Review render at `:832`: passes `claimAnswers`)
- Modify: `frontend/src/constants/copy.ts`, `frontend/src/test/upl-claims-copy.test.ts`
- Create: `backend/apps/intake/tests/test_claims_frontend_copy.py`: pins the four word records' keys, and `claimNames.ts`'s `DATE_INCURRED_MAX_LENGTH` and `MAX_NOTICE_PARTIES` against `DebtInfo`'s.
- Modify: `frontend/src/components/wizard/steps/__tests__/ReviewStep.test.tsx` (the assertions at `:62`, `:103` and `:105`, in the tests that start at `:38` and `:75`)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: Tasks 8–10's steps, each holding its list inline (`CONTRACT_TYPES`, `LEASE_KIND_OPTIONS`, `RELATIONSHIPS`, and `DEBT_CATEGORY_META`'s labels), and Task 7's `claimAnswers` state in `IntakeWizard`.
- Produces:
  - `DEBT_TYPE_WORDS`, `CONTRACT_TYPE_WORDS`, `CODEBTOR_RELATIONSHIP_WORDS` and `LEASE_KIND_WORDS` in `claims.ts`, keyed as the server's choices, in their order.
  - `ReviewStep` prop `claimAnswers?: ClaimAnswers` (default `{}`).
- **Words kept as they are today** (this task moves them, it doesn't reword them): the debt labels of `DEBT_CATEGORY_META`, `CONTRACT_TYPES` and `RELATIONSHIPS` as the steps hold them, and Task 9's lease kinds.

**`claims.ts`'s exact layout.** Prettier's, so the pin's regex reads it. Each record opens with `export const NAME…= {` on one line, holds one entry per line indented two spaces as `key: 'Words',` (single quotes, a trailing comma, no apostrophe in a word, under 100 columns so prettier never wraps a value), and closes with `};` alone at column 0:

```ts
/**
 * The words the claim steps and Review use for the server's choices. The server holds the keys
 * in `apps/intake/models.py`; `test_claims_frontend_copy.py` pins the keys, in order.
 */
import type { CodebtorRelationship, ContractType, DebtInfo, ExecutoryContract } from '../types/api';

export const DEBT_TYPE_WORDS: Record<DebtInfo['debt_type'], string> = {
  credit_card: 'Credit card',
  medical: 'Medical bill',
  personal_loan: 'Personal loan',
  student_loan: 'Student loan',
  auto_loan: 'Car or vehicle loan',
  mortgage: 'Mortgage or home loan',
  utility: 'Utility bill',
  other: 'Other amount owed',
};

export const CONTRACT_TYPE_WORDS: Record<ContractType, string> = {
  lease: 'Lease',
  contract: 'Executory Contract',
  other: 'Other',
};

export const CODEBTOR_RELATIONSHIP_WORDS: Record<CodebtorRelationship, string> = {
  spouse: 'Spouse',
  former_spouse: 'Former Spouse',
  relative: 'Relative',
  friend: 'Friend',
  business_partner: 'Business Partner',
  other: 'Other',
};

export const LEASE_KIND_WORDS: Record<NonNullable<ExecutoryContract['lease_kind']>, string> = {
  residential: 'Residential',
  vehicle: 'Vehicle',
  other_personal_property: 'Other personal property',
  other: 'Other',
};
```

Two things in that layout matter to the pin. A `Record<…, …>` type may span several words but sits on the `export const` line, so the regex starts at `export const NAME` and runs to the first `^};`. The entries' indent is exactly two spaces. If Task 7 gave `lease_kind` another type, keep the `Record` key type and the entries. `ContractType` and `CodebtorRelationship` are Task 7's types without the blank `''` that `ExecutoryContract['contract_type']` and `Codebtor['relationship']` allow: a record typed by the widened field would need a `''` key and fail `tsc`. Review indexes the two records only after a non-blank guard.

**Review's words.**

- A debt row reads `{creditor_name || 'Unnamed Creditor'}` and, when it has a type, ` ({DEBT_TYPE_WORDS[debt_type]})`. A contract reads `{counterparty_name || REVIEW_UNNAMED_CONTRACT}` and ` ({CONTRACT_TYPE_WORDS[contract_type]})` when it has a type. A codebtor reads `{name || REVIEW_UNNAMED_CODEBTOR}` and ` ({CODEBTOR_RELATIONSHIP_WORDS[relationship]})` when it has one. A blank type, or a value `claims.ts` doesn't hold (an old row), shows no parentheses, never `undefined` or a raw key.
- Order is what the wizard holds (R19): `ordered_debts`' order as the session read gave it, then rows added since. No sort is added.
- Empty lists: with `claimAnswers.no_debts === true`, `REVIEW_NO_DEBTS`; with `contracts_answer === 'no'`, `REVIEW_NO_CONTRACTS`; with `codebtors_answer === 'no'`, `REVIEW_NO_CODEBTORS`. Otherwise today's `No amounts owed listed.`, `No contracts or leases listed.` and `No codebtors listed.` stand. With rows listed, the sentence never shows.
- `copy.ts`, none a draft; each states what the filer pressed, never what it means for the case:
  - `REVIEW_NO_DEBTS = 'You said you have no amounts owed.'`
  - `REVIEW_NO_CONTRACTS = 'You said you have no contracts or leases.'`
  - `REVIEW_NO_CODEBTORS = 'You said you have no codebtors.'`
  - `REVIEW_UNNAMED_CONTRACT = 'Unnamed contract'`
  - `REVIEW_UNNAMED_CODEBTOR = 'Unnamed codebtor'`

Open that file and copy its imports and skip line, then:

```python
from apps.intake.models import Codebtor, DebtInfo, ExecutoryContract


def _keys(name: str) -> list[str]:
    """The keys of one `export const NAME … = {` record in constants/claims.ts, in order."""
    text = (FRONTEND / "constants" / "claims.ts").read_text()
    match = re.search(rf"^export const {name}\b[^\n]*= \{{\n(.*?)^\}};", text, re.M | re.S)
    assert match, f"claims.ts has no {name}"
    return re.findall(r"^  (\w+): '[^'\n]+',$", match.group(1), re.M)


def test_the_debt_kinds_the_wizard_offers_are_the_servers():
    assert _keys("DEBT_TYPE_WORDS") == [key for key, _ in DebtInfo.DEBT_TYPE_CHOICES]


def test_the_contract_kinds_the_wizard_offers_are_the_servers():
    assert _keys("CONTRACT_TYPE_WORDS") == [
        key for key, _ in ExecutoryContract.CONTRACT_TYPE_CHOICES
    ]


def test_the_codebtor_relationships_the_wizard_offers_are_the_servers():
    assert _keys("CODEBTOR_RELATIONSHIP_WORDS") == [
        key for key, _ in Codebtor.RELATIONSHIP_CHOICES
    ]


def test_the_lease_kinds_the_wizard_offers_are_the_servers():
    assert _keys("LEASE_KIND_WORDS") == list(ExecutoryContract.LEASE_KINDS)


def _declared_integer(name: str) -> int:
    """The number of one `export const NAME = <integer>;` line in steps/claims/claimNames.ts."""
    path = FRONTEND / "components" / "wizard" / "steps" / "claims" / "claimNames.ts"
    match = re.search(rf"^export const {name} = (\d+);$", path.read_text(), re.M)
    assert match, f"claimNames.ts has no {name}"
    return int(match.group(1))


def test_the_wizard_stops_a_date_or_a_party_list_where_the_server_does():
    # A box that lets a filer type past the server's bound, or add one party too many, gets a 400
    assert _declared_integer("DATE_INCURRED_MAX_LENGTH") == DebtInfo.DATE_INCURRED_MAX_LENGTH
    assert _declared_integer("MAX_NOTICE_PARTIES") == DebtInfo.MAX_NOTICE_PARTIES
```

  - Each test would fail if a key were reordered, renamed or dropped on either side. Their docstring says why: a kind spelled differently on one side makes every save of that row a 400, or a Review that shows a raw key.
  - `DebtInfo.DEBT_TYPE_CHOICES` is a `ClassVar` list (`models.py:765`), `ExecutoryContract.CONTRACT_TYPE_CHOICES` and `Codebtor.RELATIONSHIP_CHOICES` plain class lists (`:1015`, `:1041`), and `LEASE_KINDS` is Task 1's tuple of keys. Check the last by opening `models.py`; if Task 1 made it a list of pairs, take the keys as the first three lines do.
  - `_keys` asserts its record is found, so a regex that matches nothing fails instead of comparing two empty lists. `_declared_integer` does the same, and reads the one-line declarations Task 8 makes in `claimNames.ts`. `DebtInfo.DATE_INCURRED_MAX_LENGTH` and `DebtInfo.MAX_NOTICE_PARTIES` are Task 1's class attributes.

A 15-character date on a D row can still be `ValueTooLong` at print time, and the print path already says so with `step: "debts"` (§7.2).

**Persona data shape.** Each persona's `debts` entries keep today's keys and gain the keys below (the same names as the model), and the persona gains `contracts`, `codebtors` and `claims`:

- A debt: `street_address`, `street_2`, `city`, `state`, `zip_code`, `account_number`, `date_incurred`, `claim_status` (a list), `offset_answer`, `notice_parties` (rows without ids), and, on a secured row, `lien` (a dict), `link` (an A/B position such as `"3.1"`) and, for DeShawn, `home_payment_line`. `link` is the one key that is not a column: the seed and the readback resolve it with `asset_at`.
- A contract: the model's names, plus `vehicle_link` (an A/B position, Andrés's Corolla only).
- A codebtor: the model's names, plus `links`, as `[("debt", name)]` pairs for `claim_links`.
- `claims`: the `ClaimAnswers` keys of the table.
- Helpers in the seed, so each debt stays one short literal: `_claim(street, city, state, zip_code, *, last_4="", date="", street_2="", status=(), offset="no", notice=(), link=None, lien=None, **more) -> dict` returns the keys above, and `_party(name, street, city, state, zip_code, last_4) -> dict` is a notice-party row. A debt is `{**existing keys, **_claim(…)}`.

```python
@pytest.mark.django_db
@pytest.mark.parametrize("persona", list(PERSONAS))
def test_every_persona_answers_every_claim_question(persona):
    # Seeded before 3c3, a persona is refused until it is re-seeded; seeded now, none is.
    call_command("loaddata", "ilnd_2025_data", verbosity=0)
    call_command("seed_demo_data", persona=persona, stdout=StringIO())
    session = IntakeSession.objects.get(user__username=f"demo_{persona}")
    assert [str(gap) for gap in claim_step_gaps(session)] == []


@pytest.mark.django_db
def test_priyas_car_loan_links_to_her_car():
    call_command("loaddata", "ilnd_2025_data", verbosity=0)
    call_command("seed_demo_data", persona="priya", stdout=StringIO())
    session = IntakeSession.objects.get(user__username="demo_priya")
    kestrel = session.debts.get(creditor_name="Kestrel Auto Finance")
    car = rows_on(ordered_assets(session), "3")[0]
    assert kestrel.secured_by_id == car.pk
    assert (kestrel.amount_owed, kestrel.monthly_payment) == (Decimal("14900.00"), Decimal("450.00"))
    assert car.get_details()["model"] == "CR-V EX"
    assert session.debts.count() == 3

@pytest.mark.django_db
@pytest.mark.parametrize("persona", list(PERSONAS))
def test_each_personas_dates_fit_their_boxes(persona):
    # A date too long for its box is ValueTooLong on the form it prints on (S16). D prints only
    # a secured debt's date, in its 50 pt box; E/F prints the rest, in the narrowest of its boxes.
    for debt in PERSONAS[persona]()["debts"]:
        form, key = (
            ("schedule_d", "Date debt was incurred@1:T31")
            if debt.get("is_secured")
            else ("schedule_e_f", "Date debt was incurred@1:T14")
        )
        PDFFormFiller().check(form, {key: debt["date_incurred"]})  # raises ValueTooLong
```

### Task 13: Journeys

**Files:**

- Modify: `frontend/e2e/fixtures/personas.ts`:
  - `PersonaDebt` gains the address, answers, link (by A/B position) and notice parties.

- Modify: `frontend/e2e/pages/wizard.page.ts`:
  - `addDebt` gains every new input (§7.7; it keeps `debt_type_<i>`, `creditor_name_<i>`, `amount_owed_<i>`, `monthly_payment_<i>` and `collateral_description_<i>`).
  - New: `answerNoDebts`, `walkDebts(persona)`, `walkContracts(persona)`, `walkCodebtors(persona)`, `fillDebtAnswers(index, debt)` (what `addDebt` fills after the type, creditor and amount, which a scanned draft already holds), and `answerLivesHere` within `addAsset`.
  - The page object's `addAsset` special-cases `lives_here` as a radio, as it does `who`.
- Modify every journey in `frontend/e2e/journeys/`: steps 7 and 8 are answered, not skipped.
  - `maria-happy-path`, `james-borderline`, `priya-ineligible`, `deshawn-assets`, `sarah-simplest`, `andres-gig-driver` and `sofa-journey` replace their debt loop, their Continue, and their two empty Continues with `walkDebts`, `walkContracts` and `walkCodebtors`.
  - `deshawn-assets` answers `line_1_0_lives_here` through his home's `lives_here` detail (no journey edit beyond the fixture).
  - `scanned-bill-journey` fills its draft's address and answers before Continue (§7.7), and its header comment's lines 27-29 ("Saving debts clears is_draft (intake/views.py), so the badge is gone once step 6 has saved (DIG-14)") become "Continue on Amounts Owed (`update_step` 7) confirms a draft, so the badge is gone once Continue has been pressed (DIG-14)".

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: Task 5's `name=line_<key>_<i>_lives_here` radios (values `yes` and `no`), Tasks 8–10's input names (listed in the skeleton), and Task 12's persona data.
- Produces: nothing later tasks read. The controller runs `maria-happy-path` locally; CI runs all eight.

**What the page object assumes of Tasks 8–10.** The skeleton fixes every input's `name` but not its control or its words. This task is written to these, and Tasks 8–10 must match them (a mismatch is a one-line change here):

- Radios: `is_secured_<i>`, `offset_answer_<i>`, `notice_<i>`, `home_payment_line_<i>`, `collateral_who_<i>` (values `yes`/`no`, `first`/`additional`, `only_you`/`with_someone`), `contracts_answer`, `codebtors_answer`, `community_lived`, `community_spouse_lived_with`, `owes_now_<i>`. All are `YesNoField`-style radios valued as Tasks 8 to 10 name them (`yes`/`no`, `first`/`additional`, `only_you`/`with_someone`).
- Selects: `debt_type_<i>`, `secured_by_<i>`, `contract_type_<i>`, `lease_kind_<i>` (a select valued by the server's key, as Task 9 builds it, not a radio), `vehicle_asset_<i>`, `owed_debt_<i>`, `codebtor_relationship_<i>`. Each opens on a placeholder with no value. A picker's option text names the row in its own words (a line 3 row by `[year, make, model]` joined with spaces, the blanks skipped, which is `assetLabel`'s name for a vehicle row in Tasks 8 and 9, so `'CR-V'`, `'Fit'` and `'Corolla'` match; a line 1 row by street; a debt by creditor), so the page object picks by a substring of the option text (`pick`).
- Checkboxes: `lien_<i>` (valued `agreement`, `statutory`, `judgment`, `other`), `claim_status_<i>` (valued `contingent`, `unliquidated`, `disputed`, and `none`), `outside_us_<i>` and `notice_<i>_<j>_outside_us`, `codebtor_links_<i>` (labelled with the debt's or contract's name).
- Buttons: "I have no amounts owed" (a button named exactly that, as "No one else lives with me or depends on me." is, `wizard.page.ts:140`), "Add Another Amount Owed", which `addDebt` already uses and Task 8 keeps, and the existing "+ Add Contract" and "+ Add Codebtor" (Tasks 9 and 10 keep those words; matched as `/add contract/i` and `/add codebtor/i`). Contracts and codebtors show no blank row, so those two are pressed for every row, the first included.
- "Yes" on `notice_<i>` opens the first party at `<j>` = 0; the personas have at most one party on a debt, so no "Add another" press is needed.
- The contracts' and codebtors' address inputs are named as Task 8's for a contract (`street_address_<i>`, `street_2_<i>`, `city_<i>`, `state_<i>`, `zip_code_<i>`, `outside_us_<i>`) and prefixed `codebtor_` for a codebtor (`codebtor_street_address_<i>`, and so on); H line 2's are prefixed `community_spouse_` (`community_spouse_street_address`, …).

```ts
/** A synthetic address as the claims steps ask it; `outside_us` is ticked, never assumed */
export interface PersonaAddress {
  street_address: string;
  street_2?: string;
  city: string;
  state: string;
  zip_code: string;
  outside_us?: boolean;
}

export interface PersonaNoticeParty extends PersonaAddress {
  name: string;
  last_4: string;
}

export type ClaimStatusTick = 'contingent' | 'unliquidated' | 'disputed';
export type LienKind = 'agreement' | 'statutory' | 'judgment' | 'other';

/** What a secured debt asks: its property, by A/B position and by the picker's words, and its lien */
export interface PersonaSecured {
  /** The A/B row's place in the walk, as seed_demo_data's `link`: line "3", second row is "3.2" */
  position: string;
  /** A substring of the picker's option text for that row (its make and model, or its street) */
  pick: string;
  lien: LienKind[];
  home_payment_line?: 'first' | 'additional';
  collateral_who?: 'only_you' | 'with_someone';
}

export interface PersonaContract {
  counterparty_name: string;
  contract_type: 'lease' | 'contract' | 'other';
  description: string;
  address: PersonaAddress;
  lease_kind?: 'residential' | 'vehicle' | 'other_personal_property' | 'other';
  /** A vehicle lease's A/B row: its position and a substring of the picker's option text */
  vehicle?: { position: string; pick: string };
  monthly_payment?: number;
  owes_now: 'yes' | 'no';
}

export interface PersonaCodebtor {
  name: string;
  relationship?: string;
  address: PersonaAddress;
  /** The names the row shares: the creditor of a debt or the other party of a contract */
  links: string[];
}

/** The answers asked on steps 6–8 beside the rows (R16), as the seed's `claims` */
export interface PersonaClaimAnswers {
  contracts_answer: 'yes' | 'no';
  codebtors_answer: 'yes' | 'no';
  community_lived: 'yes' | 'no';
}
```

A nested debt row takes every answer the Amounts Owed step asks (3c3), a half-filled row included: a blank `creditor_name` or `debt_type` saves, `amount_owed` stays non-null and a blank one is 0 (never an "I don't know"), and a negative is a 400 on every path. The row's address columns, `outside_us`, `date_incurred`, `home_payment_line`, `collateral_who`, `offset_answer`, `community_debt`, `lien`, `claim_status` and `notice_parties` save with it (`notice_parties` keep never-reused ids, at most 10 a debt), and `account_last_4` is read-only, the last four digits only. `secured_by` is an id or null: `_save_rows_by_id(…, scoped=…)` checks it against this session's assets, and an id that names no row of the session saves as no link and is never echoed. A removed property sets it null (`SET_NULL`). An asset row is a Schedule A/B row (3b):`

  1b. Old:
  > `A new row with no name stays out of the PUT; a stored row whose name is cleared makes the step invalid, so it is never sent without its name, and never deleted by being left out.`

  New:
  > `A new row with nothing in it stays out of the PUT, and any other row goes, even with no name: a stored row whose name is cleared is sent nameless under its id, so it is kept and Complete names it (R17).`

  1c. Old:
  > ``DebtViewSet`/`AssetViewSet` keep the base serializers, so per-item create never takes a client id.`

  New:
  > `` `DebtViewSet`/`AssetViewSet` keep the base serializers, so per-item create never takes a client id, and a debt's `secured_by` and a contract's or codebtor's links are read-only on the per-item paths. ``

  1d. Old:
  > `an empty list deletes every stored row, and a GET lists them by id.`

  New:
  > `an empty list deletes every stored row, and a GET lists them by id. A contract row also takes its address, `outside_us`, `lease_kind`, `monthly_payment`, `vehicle_asset`, `owes_now` and `owed_debt`, and a codebtor row its `street_2`, `outside_us` and `links` (`[{kind, id}]`, dropped on read when the row is gone); a name or a type may be blank, and a link to another filer's row saves as no link.`

  **2. "Forms need a completed intake"** (five edits).

  2a. Old:
  > `and a scanned debt the filer hasn't confirmed (a `DebtInfo` with `is_draft`, step `debts`; see "Document upload sits mid-wizard").`

  New:
  > `then every gap of `claim_step_gaps` (`apps/intake/claims.py`, 3c3; `step` is `assets`, `debts`, `contracts` or `codebtors`): first a debt linked to a line 1 or line 4 row whose "Do you live here?" is unanswered (step `assets`), then the Debts block with a scanned debt the filer hasn't confirmed first (a `DebtInfo` with `is_draft`; see "Document upload sits mid-wizard"), the step's own gaps, and each debt's, then the contracts and codebtors blocks, each row named by its name or "one amount owed", "one contract" or "one codebtor".`

  2b. Old:
  > ``_prepare` then raises the first `AssetStepGap` (a 422 `{error, step: "assets"}`, like `IncomeStepGap`) before it works the means test out,`

  New:
  > `` `_prepare` then raises the first `AssetStepGap` (a 422 `{error, step: "assets"}`, like `IncomeStepGap`) and then the first `ClaimStepGap` (a 422 with its own `step`) before it works the means test out, ``

  2c. Old:
  > `The session's completion percentage counts the assets section done only when `asset_step_gaps` finds nothing.`

  New:
  > `The session's completion percentage counts the assets section done only when `asset_step_gaps` finds nothing, and the debts section only when `claim_step_gaps` has no gap with `step == "debts"`, so a filer who pressed "I have no amounts owed" has finished it.`

  2d. Old:
  > `a session PATCH carrying `debtor_info`, `income_info`, `expense_info`, `debts`, `assets` or `asset_lines`;`

  New:
  > `a session PATCH carrying `debtor_info`, `income_info`, `expense_info`, `debts`, `assets`, `asset_lines` or `claim_answers`;`

  2e. Old:
  > `A forms refusal's `step` also comes from `AssetStepGap.step` (`assets`) and `IncomeStepGap.step` (`income_info`).`

  New:
  > `A forms refusal's `step` also comes from `AssetStepGap.step` (`assets`), `IncomeStepGap.step` (`income_info`) and `ClaimStepGap.step` (`assets`, `debts`, `contracts` or `codebtors`, set per instance).`

  And one addition at the end of the `answer_household` sentences. Old:
  > `The writes that reopen a completed intake gain `household`, `spouse_income` and `asset_lines` in a session PATCH.`

  New:
  > `The writes that reopen a completed intake gain `household`, `spouse_income`, `asset_lines` and `claim_answers` in a session PATCH. `answer_household`'s `claims` default answers the claims steps (`no_debts` true on a session with no debt rows, and contracts, codebtors and H line 2 No); `claims=None` leaves them, and `complete_debt` (`builders.py`) gives a debt every answer and clears `no_debts`.`

  **3. "Autosave sends only real edits"** (two edits).

  3a. Old:
  > `Step 7's body is `{contracts}` and step 8's `{codebtors}`: every row with an id or a name, each keeping its `_key`, which stays out of the request, the bare list the PUT takes.`

  New:
  > `Step 7's body is `{contracts, claim_answers: {contracts_answer}}` and step 8's `{codebtors, claim_answers: {codebtors_answer, the H line 2 keys}}`: every row except an all-blank one with no id, each keeping its `_key`, which stays out of the request, the bare list the PUT takes, then the session PATCH that carries only `claim_answers`; either failing fails the save and the next save sends both again. Every `_`-prefixed key stays out of every request.`

  3b. Old:
  > `Step 6's body, Amounts Owed, sends each debt row with a blank `monthly_payment` as null, so a cleared payment clears.`

  New:
  > `Step 6's body, Amounts Owed, sends every debt row except an all-blank one with no id, a blank `monthly_payment` as null (so a cleared payment clears) and a blank amount as 0, with `claim_answers: {no_debts}` (`true` only while "I have no amounts owed" is pressed and no row is touched, null otherwise). The amount box shows a stored 0 as empty, so a visit alone sends nothing, and Debts, Contracts and Codebtors autosave while the step still has a gap.`

  **4. "Seed order"** (one addition). Old:
  > `the packet list never runs that check. Re-create them with `seed_demo_data --reset`.`

  New:
  > `the packet list never runs that check. Re-create them with `seed_demo_data --reset`. Personas seeded before 3c3 have no addresses, status, notice answer, lease or codebtor rows and no `ClaimAnswers`, so Complete and every forms call past the Assets step's check refuse them with the first claim gap (a debt's address): re-create them with `seed_demo_data --reset`. The seed writes each persona's debts with their address, last 4, date, status, offset and notice parties, Priya's Kestrel car loan, each secured debt's link to its A/B row by `ordered_assets` position (`asset_at`), DeShawn's "I live here" and first mortgage, each lease and codebtor, and the persona's `ClaimAnswers`.`

  **5. "Document upload sits mid-wizard"** (two edits).

  5a. Old:
  > `until the filer saves the Amounts Owed step, whose session PATCH carrying `debts` clears every draft of the session; Complete refuses while one remains (DIG-163).`

  New:
  > `until the filer presses Continue on the Amounts Owed step: `update_step` with 7 clears every draft of the session, under the session's lock, and no PATCH does, so an autosave never confirms a scanned row nobody read. Complete refuses while one remains (DIG-163) and names the draft's own gaps beside it (an address, an amount above $0).`

  5b. Old:
  > ``scanned-bill-journey` uploads with `uploadBill()` and clicks Continue.`

  New:
  > `` `scanned-bill-journey` uploads with `uploadBill()`, clicks Continue, and answers its draft's address and questions with `fillDebtAnswers` before Continue on Amounts Owed. ``

  **6. "The Assets step asks Schedule A/B's lines"** (one addition). Old:
  > `` `test_asset_lines_frontend_copy.py` pins both. ``

  New:
  > `` `test_asset_lines_frontend_copy.py` pins both. `details.lives_here` (`yes`, `no` or null) is asked under every line 1 row and every line 4 row (a home on wheels), as radios named `line_<key>_<i>_lives_here`; it is no Assets gap, and becomes one only once a debt links to the row (a `claim_step_gaps` gap, `step: "assets"`, named by `_row_name`). ``

  **7. Not in §7.7's list, but now stale (offer, don't force).**

  7a. "Saves lock the session", old:
  > `and the lock test's `WRITES` holds the household, income and `asset_lines` PATCHes and the filing-fee PUT.`

  New:
  > `and the lock test's `WRITES` holds the household, income, `asset_lines` and `claim_answers` PATCHes, the debts PATCH with links, Continue to step 7 (which confirms drafts under the lock) and the filing-fee PUT.`

  7b. "Debt and asset amounts are readable, as strings", old:
  > `every write path must bound it (`DraftDebtCreator` stores it as 0; documents `validate` returns 400; `AssetInfoSerializer` returns 400).`

  New:
  > `every write path must bound it (`DraftDebtCreator` stores it as 0; documents `validate` returns 400; `AssetInfoSerializer` returns 400). A debt's `account_number` stays write-only: the reply carries `account_last_4`, its last four digits and no other. A negative `amount_owed` is a 400 on the session save, the per-item save and a scanned bill's correction.`

  **8. New gotcha**, inserted before the line that starts `- **Upload areas decide the document type**`:

  > `- **The claims steps ask every answer Schedules D, E/F, G and H need, and `claim_step_gaps` refuses what is missing** — `apps/intake/claims.py` holds `ordered_debts`, `account_last_4(number)` (the last four digits of the number's digits, "" for none, which 3c5 prints), `ClaimStepGap(message, step)` and `claim_step_gaps(session)`, the one table of gaps in wizard order (`assets`: a debt linked to a line 1 or line 4 row with no `lives_here`; `debts`: the draft sentence, "no amounts owed" answered, then each debt in `ordered_debts` order, its name, kind, an amount above $0, its address, and under a secured row the property, its description, the lien, the monthly payment, first or additional, who owns it, then its status, offset (unsecured only), the notice answer and each notice party; `contracts`: the question, then each row in id order; `codebtors`: the question, H line 2, then each row in id order). It imports no model and reads the session's relations by attribute, as `asset_step_gaps` does; `address_missing` counts a state missing unless it is two letters and a ZIP missing when blank, except on an address ticked `outside_us`, and `link_is_live` counts a link live only when it names a row of the same session (a row moved to another intake is no link). Complete lists every gap, and `_prepare` raises the first; the packet list never refuses. Each gap's sentence is a one-literal constant in `frontend/src/constants/copy.ts` (`DEBT_GAP_*`, `CONTRACT_GAP_*`, `CODEBTOR_GAP_*`, `CLAIM_GAP_*`), and `test_claim_gaps.py` pins each to the server's; the steps' own copies (`debtGaps.ts`, `contractGaps.ts`, `codebtorGaps.ts`) show only the first gap and leave out the draft sentence and the Assets one. `ClaimAnswers` (one-to-one, `claim_answers`, one encrypted JSON column) holds `no_debts`, `contracts_answer`, `codebtors_answer`, H line 2's answers and the spouse's address; a session PATCH sets only the keys sent, a null clears one and an unknown key or value is a 400. The choice lists (`DEBT_TYPE_WORDS`, `CONTRACT_TYPE_WORDS`, `CODEBTOR_RELATIONSHIP_WORDS`, `LEASE_KIND_WORDS` in `frontend/src/constants/claims.ts`) are pinned by `test_claims_frontend_copy.py`; the codebtor list is not the household's `RELATIONSHIP_WORDS`. Nothing ships pre-selected except "Can the lender take back property…", which starts from the debt type until the filer presses it (`_securedPressed`, browser-only). No printed row changes in 3c3: G's and H's line 1 ticks, the `has_*` ticks and the totals follow the rows.`

  **9. File Map.** Old:
  > `- `apps/intake/claims.py` — `ordered_debts`, the order Schedules D and E/F list claims in; pure, no models`

  New:
  > `` - `apps/intake/claims.py` — `ordered_debts` (the order Schedules D and E/F list claims in), `account_last_4`, `ClaimStepGap` and `claim_step_gaps` (every gap the claims steps leave, in wizard order); no models imported ``

  And, after the `apps/intake/models.py` line, no change is needed (it already lists `FilingFee`; add `ClaimAnswers` to its list: old `AssetLineAnswers, DebtInfo, FilingFee, Household, SpouseIncome` becomes `AssetLineAnswers, ClaimAnswers, DebtInfo, FilingFee, Household, SpouseIncome`; the model list in that line is in that order).

CLAUDE.md names the new gaps, the moved draft confirmation and the claims module."

---

## Handed to 3c4 and later

- **3c4 (payments move).** It reads `secured_by`, `home_payment_line`, `lives_here`, `vehicle_asset` and a contract's `monthly_payment` as §8.1's mapping says.
  - It drops `vehicle_payment` and `other_property_mortgages` from the seed and fixtures (DIG-282).
  - The seed's `vehicle_payment` figures (350, 450, 389) stay in 3c3.
- **3c5 (rows print).** It prints `account_last_4(number)` (R20), the address columns (blank state and ZIP outside the US, B49), `claim_status`, `offset_answer`, `community_debt`, the notice parties, the leases and the codebtor links.
  - A 15-character en-dash date on a D row is `ValueTooLong` at print (R21).
- **Between 3c3 and 3c5,** a seeded session's G and H tick Yes with no row printed beneath (§4, B1). No promotion of `develop` to `main` until 3c6 merges.
- **Still open from 3c2:** one save-and-pair helper over the held lists; debts serialized two or three times per session reply.
- **`IntakeSessionSerializer.create`** writes debts with `DebtInfo.objects.create(**debt_data)`, which skips `_assign`'s JSON handling. No client creates a session with debts, so 3c3 leaves it as is.
- **Follow-ups outside 3c (for the owner):** CI runs no `makemigrations --check`.
